**Recommendation.** In the dry run (see the validation opportunity), show RM match status per row and require an explicit fallback: map to a chosen RM, or leave the case unassigned in the Collections 'Unassigned' bucket. Never default to the importer. Rename the template column to billing_date and reject bare numbers unless a separate 'days_overdue' column is used. Show a warning chip when a contractor_id doesn't resolve. After import, list the cases with rm_matched=false and add a 'Reassign' bulk action.

*Verification:* confirmed: Confirmed, and worse than described. In actions.ts:242-248, rmId = row.rm_id; a non-UUID rm_id falls back to the rm_name lookup or user.id. If rm_id is BLANK, the code goes straight to user.id and never consults rm_name. The shipped template leaves rm_id empty ('John Doe,,'), so every templated grandfathered import assigns all cases to the importing admin, even when the RM exists. rm_matched (:316) is computed from rm_name alone, so it is stored as TRUE for those misassigned cases. The importer fallback also contradicts doctrine. On top of that, CollectionsClient.getRmName (CollectionsClient.tsx:91-97) displays original_rm_name ahead of the actual rm user, so the UI shows 'John Doe' as RM while ownership, queues and notifications go to the importer. The misassignment is hidden. overdue_date is mapped to billing_date, with bare numbers read as days-ago (:251-260). An unresolved contractor becomes null and flips case_scenario to customer_pays (:238, 300). Kept at P1, borderline P0: legacy collections follow-up is systematically misrouted, with a display that masks it. The recommendation is sound. Also fix the blank-rm_id branch so it tries rm_name, and stop letting original_rm_name override the real assignee in the display.

### #46 [P1] Party Master has no search, filter or pagination, and editing an inactive party silently reactivates it
*Area:* admin-settings · *Kind:* usability · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** The Party Master tab renders up to 1000 parties in an 8-column table with no search box, no Active/Inactive/Candidate filter, no sort and no pagination. Finding one party means Ctrl-F. upsertParty always writes is_active: true, so fixing a typo on a deactivated party brings it back into case intake without the admin knowing. Yet there is no explicit Reactivate button. Candidate parties are not shown here at all; is_candidate isn't even fetched, so the merge backlog is invisible from the main party view.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:12 - select without is_candidate, .limit(1000), no search passed from page`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:120 - only a count line, no search input`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:147-195 - unpaginated 8-column table`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:38 - is_active: true hardcoded in upsert payload`

**Recommendation.** Add a search field over name, code, GSTIN and PAN, filter chips (Active / Inactive / Candidate) and server pagination, since fetchParties already takes a search param. Keep is_active out of the edit payload and add an explicit Reactivate row action. Show a 'Candidate' badge with a 'Review duplicates' link to /admin/aliases pre-filtered.

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Confirmed: fetchParties selects without is_candidate and uses .limit(1000) (actions.ts:12). page.tsx calls fetchParties() with no search, and the search param only does ilike on legal_name. The tab shows a count line but no search, filters, sort or pagination (AdminClient.tsx:120, 147-195). upsertParty hardcodes is_active: true (actions.ts:38), so editing a deactivated party silently reactivates it, and there is no explicit Reactivate action. Partly wrong: 'Candidate parties are not shown here at all.' Candidates ARE listed; nothing filters them out. They just can't be told apart, because is_candidate isn't fetched. Extra: upsertParty's update branch ignores the Supabase error (:41), so a failed edit still reports success and logs 'saved'. The recommendation is sound. Extend the server search beyond legal_name (code, GSTIN, PAN).

### #47 [P1] User creation uses admin-typed passwords, raw role slugs, a non-resetting form and undocumented partial failure
*Area:* admin-settings · *Kind:* usability · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** To onboard someone, the admin types a password for them; the placeholder 'Password123' suggests a weak one. Nothing forces a reset on first sign-in, and the email is auto-confirmed. Roles appear as raw slugs ('ordinary_approver', 'founder_admin') in both dropdowns and badges, with no description of what each grants. After a successful create, the inline form keeps its values, which invites duplicate submits. The action creates the auth user first and then the profile and role, so a later failure leaves an orphaned login with no role. The admin sees only a raw DB error (doctrine 12.13: 'User creation should explain partial-failure behavior'). The user table has no status, last sign-in or 'no roles' indicator, so dormant or role-less accounts can't be found.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:217-220 - Password field with placeholder 'Password123'`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:196-201 - createUser with admin-supplied password, email_confirm: true`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:19, 224, 263-265, 275 - raw role slugs everywhere`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:52-63 - success path does not reset the form`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:203-226 - sequential auth to profile to role writes, no rollback of the auth user on later failure`

**Recommendation.** Replace the password field with 'Send invite' (supabase.auth.admin.inviteUserByEmail), or at least generate a one-time password with a forced reset. Add a ROLE_LABELS map with one-line descriptions (e.g. 'Ordinary approver: reviews assigned stages') and use it everywhere. Reset the form and open the new user's row after creation. On a profile or role failure, delete the auth user and return a clear message. Add 'Last sign-in' and a 'No role' warning badge.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. The password input uses placeholder 'Password123' (AdminClient.tsx:217-220). createUser takes the admin-supplied password with email_confirm: true (actions.ts:196-201). Raw role slugs appear in the ROLES array, both selects and the badges (:19, 224, 263-265, 275). The badge's 'capitalize' class only produces 'Ordinary_approver'. On success the handler calls setUserOpen(false), but the form is inline and userOpen controls nothing, so the fields keep their values and nothing resets (:52-63). The writes run in order auth user, then profile, then role, with no rollback of the auth user (:203-226), so a failure leaves a login with no role (it resolves to 'viewer'), and the admin sees a raw error. The table has no last sign-in or no-role indicator. The recommendation is sound.

### #48 [P1] Audit log is not human-readable: raw UUIDs, unlinked entities, no before/after, no actor role
*Area:* admin-settings · *Kind:* clarity · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin, board_member

**Problem.** Audit descriptions insert raw IDs: 'Role 'rm' assigned to 3f9c…', 'Party 7a1e… deactivated', 'Deleted user 9b2d…', 'Grandfathered via import job e41c…'. The case reference is a truncated 8-character UUID that isn't a link. Structured field_diffs are captured but the global log never renders them; only the per-case AuditTab does. The actor's role isn't shown either. An admin answering 'who changed this party, and from what to what?' can't do it from this screen, which fails the Principle 18 design test outright.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:64 - `Party ${id} deactivated.``
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:88, 105 - role events reference target userId UUID`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:261 - `Deleted user ${targetUserId}` (name is unrecoverable once the user is deleted)`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:45, 49 - party edits logged with no field_diffs`
- `src/app/audit/page.tsx:38 - `Case ${e.case_id.slice(0, 8)}...` plain text, not a link`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/AuditTab.tsx:112 - field_diffs rendering exists but is not reused on /audit`

**Recommendation.** Write human names into description and metadata at write time: target user name and email, party legal name and code, case number. For user deletion this is mandatory, since the profile disappears. Log field_diffs for party edits, role changes and setting changes. On /audit, link case_number to /cases/[id] and party to its record. Reuse the AuditTab diff renderer ('₹X → ₹Y'), add the actor's active role, and put raw JSON behind a 'Technical details' disclosure.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. `Party ${id} deactivated.` (actions.ts:64). The role events log the target userId UUID (:88, :105). `Deleted user ${targetUserId}` (:261). The /audit case reference is plain text `Case ${e.case_id.slice(0, 8)}...` (audit/page.tsx:38). AuditTab renders field_diffs via humanizeDiff (AuditTab.tsx:~111-115), but /audit does not reuse it. Correction: party edits DO include the legal name in the description (`Party '${payload.legal_name}' saved.`, :45/:49). Only field_diffs is missing for party edits. Also, given the FK behaviour, user deletes rarely succeed, but when one does the name is lost, so writing names at write time remains mandatory. The recommendation is sound.

### #49 [P1] Audit log can't be searched or filtered, is hard-capped, duplicated across two screens, and shows times in the server's timezone
*Area:* admin-settings · *Kind:* efficiency · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** /audit shows the latest 200 events and nothing older: no pagination, no filters (actor, event type, date range, case or party), no search and no export. During an investigation, events older than about a day of activity simply can't be reached. The Admin panel has a second 'Global Audit Log' tab with 50 events, different styling and raw snake_case event types, so admins don't know which one is authoritative. Timestamps use new Date().toLocaleString() inside a Server Component, so they render in the server's locale and timezone (UTC on most hosts), not IST. Settings on the same back office uses formatDateTimeIST. A 14:22 IST event shows as 08:52, which is misleading during a dispute.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/audit/page.tsx:7 - fetchGlobalAuditLog(200), no params`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:113-121 - fetchGlobalAuditLog supports only a limit, and has no admin check`
- `src/app/audit/page.tsx:42 - new Date(e.created_at).toLocaleString() on the server`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:373-397 - duplicate audit tab (50 events, event_type shown raw at :387, toLocaleString at :390)`
- `src/app/settings/page.tsx:83 - formatDateTimeIST is the established convention`

**Recommendation.** Make /audit the single audit surface and remove the Admin tab, or turn it into a link. Add URL-driven filters (actor, event type multi-select, date range, case number / party search), cursor pagination and a CSV export of the filtered set. Use formatDateTimeIST for every timestamp, with relative time on hover. Add an admin check to fetchGlobalAuditLog.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. /audit calls fetchGlobalAuditLog(200) (audit/page.tsx:7). fetchGlobalAuditLog takes only a limit and has no role check (actions.ts:113-121). RLS 'auth_read'/'read_audit' lets any authenticated user read audit_events anyway, and /audit has no page-level gate. The Admin tab is a duplicate: 50 events, raw event_type at :387, toLocaleString at :390. Nuance on timezone: /audit is a Server Component, so toLocaleString there renders in the server's TZ/locale (confirmed misleading). AdminClient and ImportsClient are client components, so they end up in the browser's TZ after hydration, with a possible hydration mismatch. The admin tab is less wrong than claimed. formatDateTimeIST exists (lib/format.ts:38) and Settings uses it (settings/page.tsx:83). The recommendation is sound.

### #50 [P1] Settings changes are unconfirmed, give no success receipt, and identity/vocabulary edits are never audited
*Area:* admin-settings · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** Risk controls such as the write-off slippage % and max tranche extension days 'affect live business logic immediately', but Save commits on Enter or click with no 'old → new' confirmation and no success message. The page just re-renders. Invalid input throws from the server action, so the user hits an error boundary instead of an inline message. Every change in settings/actions.ts goes unaudited: ID prefixes, city codes, and adding, toggling or deleting credit/delay reasons. A prefix change that alters every future case ID leaves no trace. Developer copy leaks into the admin UI: 'No description registered — add it to settingMeta.' and 'Blast radius is not yet registered for this control.'

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/settings/page.tsx:77-80 - plain form action, no confirm, no pending state, no toast`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/billing-actions.ts:740-745 - throws on invalid value / non-admin instead of returning an error`
- `src/app/settings/actions.ts:60-158 - no logAuditEvent call anywhere in the file`
- `src/app/settings/page.tsx:74, 82 - developer-facing fallback copy shown to admins`

**Recommendation.** Give each risk control a client form that shows 'Changing 15% → 10%: N cases pending write-off will be re-evaluated' in a confirm dialog before saving. Return {success, error}, show a toast receipt and keep errors inline. Add logAuditEvent with field_diffs {before, after} to every settings action. Replace the developer fallback copy with neutral text, and fail a unit test when a system_settings key has no settingMeta entry.

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Confirmed: the risk-control form is a plain server form action with no confirm, pending state or toast (settings/page.tsx:77-80). updateSystemSetting throws on an invalid value or a non-admin user instead of returning an error (billing-actions.ts:~740-745), which lands on the error boundary. settings/actions.ts has no logAuditEvent anywhere, so prefix, city-code and reason add/toggle/delete are unaudited. Developer copy leaks at :74 and :82. Correction: risk-control changes ARE audited. updateSystemSetting logs 'setting_updated' with the new value (billing-actions.ts, after the update), but it records no before value or field_diffs. The 'every change unaudited' scope is accurate only for settings/actions.ts. The recommendation should be to add the before value to the existing event, not a new one. One correctness note: the prefix change affects every future entity ID (idEngine.getPrefix). The title's focus on identity edits is well placed.

### #51 [P1] Login/reset flows: public self-signup on an internal tool, swallowed error messages, raw Supabase errors, alert() success
*Area:* admin-settings · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* rm, kam, accounts, bdo, ordinary_approver, board_member, founder_admin

**Problem.** Anyone can 'Sign up' for CreditFlow. The new account has no role and resolves to 'viewer', a dead end with no 'access pending' explanation, and admins aren't notified to grant a role. The callback route and the server actions redirect to /login?error=…, but the login page never reads searchParams. An expired reset link or a failed Google sign-in therefore lands on a blank sign-in card with no message. Even if it were read, the callback error always says 'Could not authenticate with Google', including for password-reset links. Supabase errors appear verbatim. The reset page doesn't check for a valid recovery session before showing the form, so users with an expired link type a new password twice and only then see a raw 'Auth session missing'. Success is a blocking alert(). Inputs lack autoComplete hints (current-password / new-password), errors aren't announced (no role=alert), and the decorative Squares background animates with no reduced-motion fallback.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/login/page.tsx:28-31, 147-153 - self sign-up path and toggle`
- `src/utils/auth-actions.ts:31 - role-less user resolves to 'viewer'`
- `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts:28 - '?error=Could not authenticate with Google' for every failure, including reset links`
- `src/app/login/actions.ts:18, 40 - more ?error redirects`
- `src/app/login/page.tsx:14-21 - page never reads useSearchParams, so the error is never shown`
- `src/app/login/page.tsx:45, 100 - raw err.message rendered; no role='alert'`
- `src/app/login/page.tsx:91, 96 and src/app/reset-password/page.tsx:70, 74 - no autoComplete attributes`
- `src/app/reset-password/page.tsx:18-47 - no session pre-check; alert() at :39`
- `src/app/login/page.tsx:12, 70 - decorative Squares animation; :114 'Or continue with' uses bg-background on a bg-card surface`

**Recommendation.** Remove self-signup, or gate it behind an 'approved domain + access request' that notifies founder_admin and shows a 'Your access is pending' page for role-less users. Read ?error on /login, map known codes to plain messages ('That reset link has expired. Request a new one.'), and pass a context-specific code from the callback. On /reset-password, check getSession() on mount and show the expired state with a 'Send a new link' button before rendering the form. Replace alert() with a redirect to /login?reset=success or a toast, add autoComplete attributes and role='alert', respect prefers-reduced-motion, and fix the divider background to bg-card.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. Public signUp is at login/page.tsx:28-31, with the toggle at :147-153. A role-less user resolves to 'viewer' (auth-actions.ts:31) and there is no pending-access page (only app/unauthorized.tsx). The callback route returns 'Could not authenticate with Google' for every failure (route.ts:28), and password reset uses the same callback (login/page.tsx:34), so reset failures get the Google message. login/actions.ts redirects with ?error (:18, :40). The login page never reads searchParams, so no message appears. err.message is rendered raw, with no role='alert' (:45, :100). There are no autoComplete attributes (:91, :96; reset-password :70, :74). The reset page has no session pre-check and uses alert() (:39). The Squares component has no prefers-reduced-motion handling (grep found none). The divider uses bg-background on a card (:114). Note: login/actions.ts login/demoLogin appear to be legacy, since the page uses the client SDK, so those redirects may never fire in practice. The callback redirect does fire. The recommendation is sound.

### #52 [P1] Irreversible money and authority actions skip ConfirmDialog: one-click write-off approval, user deletion, role revocation, policy deletes
*Area:* design-system · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin, kam, accounts

**Problem.** ConfirmDialog is used on only 3 surfaces (CaseWorkspace, PolicyVersionActions, CaseLifecycleActions). The highest-consequence actions in the product fire on a single click with no preview, no reason and no undo. 'Approve Write-Off' and credit-note 'Approve' change the ledger in one click. 'Delete user' hard-deletes the Supabase auth account. Clicking a role badge silently revokes that role, and the only hint is a hover-only title. Deleting a policy band, parameter, validity, routing or dominance rule has no confirmation and no success feedback. Elsewhere the native confirm() dialog is used for deleting a payment and for closing a case. It is unstyled, can't list consequences, and is suppressed in some browsers. Cancel-billing goes further: it writes a made-up reason into the audit trail when the user leaves the field blank. An admin moving quickly through a table can approve a shortfall write-off or remove a board member's authority by mistake, with no consequence preview.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:336-341 — Approve Write-Off is a bare submit button with no confirmation or reason`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:697-703 — credit note Approve is one click`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:280-286 + src/app/admin/actions.ts:236-263 — one-click auth.admin.deleteUser with no self-delete guard`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:258-265 — role badge inside a submit button revokes the role on click ('Click to revoke' title only; hover-only affordance)`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:186-189 — deactivate party is one click`
- `src/app/policy/bands/BandsClient.tsx:107-110, src/app/policy/parameters/ParametersClient.tsx:223-226, src/app/policy/validity/ValidityClient.tsx:114-118, src/app/policy/routing/RoutingClient.tsx:146-150, src/app/policy/dominance/DominanceClient.tsx:114 — immediate delete; src/app/policy/actions.ts:513-521 deleteScoreBand returns void and writes no audit event`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:579 and :603 — native confirm() for deleting a payment and closing a case`
- `src/app/settings/CityCodeManager.tsx:36, src/app/settings/RcaReasonManager.tsx:43, src/app/settings/DelayReasonManager.tsx:43 — native confirm()`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:785 — fd.set('reason', cancelReason || 'Order cancelled before dispatch.') writes a fabricated reason to the audit trail`

**Recommendation.** Make ConfirmDialog the only path for destructive, financial and authority actions. Write-off and credit-note approval: list the consequences (shortfall amount, new outstanding balance, closure effect) and require a reason. User delete: type-to-confirm the email, block self-deletion, and prefer deactivation. Role revoke: replace the badge-click with an explicit 'x' button labelled 'Remove KAM role from Priya' that opens a ConfirmDialog. Policy-row delete: confirm, then toast with Undo, since these are draft-only. Remove the fabricated cancel reason and make the reason required. Add an ESLint rule banning window.confirm and alert.

*Verification:* confirmed: All cited facts verified. LedgerTab.tsx:336-341 and :697-703: write-off and credit-note Approve are bare submit Buttons bound straight to server actions, with no confirm and no reason. AdminClient.tsx:258-265: the role Badge sits inside a submit <button title='Click to revoke'>. :280-286: the Trash icon runs adminDeleteUser in one click. actions.ts:236-263 calls auth.admin.deleteUser with no self-delete check, though it does write an audit event. Policy deletes (Bands, Parameters, Validity, Routing, Dominance) are bare <form action={delete...}> forms. deleteScoreBand (policy/actions.ts:513-521) writes no audit event. Native confirm() is used at LedgerTab:579 and :603 and in the three settings managers. LedgerTab:785 does write the fabricated 'Order cancelled before dispatch.' reason. ConfirmDialog is used only in CaseWorkspace, PolicyVersionActions and CaseLifecycleActions. Mitigations: handleRoleAction shows a sonner toast after admin actions; the write-off banner shows the shortfall right above the button; all of these are admin-only and rare. That supports P1 rather than P0: an admin decided to act, and nothing here misleads a credit decision. The P0-grade parts are the role-badge revoke, an affordance that looks like a label, and the irreversible auth deletion. The recommendation is sound. ConfirmDialog already supports requireMatchText, requireReason and consequences, so the fix is mostly wiring.

### #53 [P1] Base severity shades used as text fail contrast, including inside the shared StatusBadge, Badge and WorkItemRow primitives
*Area:* design-system · *Kind:* accessibility · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* rm, kam, accounts, bdo, ordinary_approver, board_member, founder_admin

**Problem.** globals.css defines -strong shades specifically as 'legible on white or on the /10 tint of its own hue (WCAG AA)'. The shared primitives that render status on every screen still use the base 500-level shade as text. Approximate contrast on white: amber-500 about 2.1:1, green-500 about 2.3:1, orange-500 about 2.8:1, red-500 about 3.8:1, all below the 4.5:1 AA threshold for normal text. On a 15% tint it is lower still. Text classes using a base severity shade appear about 137 times, against about 37 that use -strong. The 'Awaiting Input', 'Awaiting Approval' and 'Approved' pills, the WorkItemRow 'Due tomorrow' chip and the MetricCard warning value are therefore hard to read, and those are exactly the signals a user scans for. Several approve buttons also put near-white primary-foreground text on bg-success.

**Evidence.**
- `src/components/creditflow/StatusBadge.tsx:16-20 — waiting, positive, active, negative and exception families use text-warning, text-success, text-info and so on, not -strong`
- `src/components/ui/badge.tsx:13-19 — success, warning, info, attention and destructive variants use base text`
- `src/components/creditflow/WorkItemRow.tsx:57 — due chip 'bg-warning/15 text-warning' at 10px`
- `src/components/creditflow/MetricCard.tsx:35-39 — tone values use text-warning, text-destructive, text-success`
- `src/app/globals.css:88-92 — legacy .badge.* classes use base text`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/CaseWorkspace.tsx:80 — Decision tab count 'bg-warning/20 text-warning text-[10px]'`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:284, :292 — tranche chip and 'approved terms' in text-warning / text-success`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:339, :701; src/app/admin/imports/ImportsClient.tsx:162 — className='bg-success' buttons with default white foreground`

**Recommendation.** Change StatusBadge FAMILY_CLASSES, the Badge variants, WorkItemRow, MetricCard and the legacy .badge classes to text-{tone}-strong. Add a destructive.strong-backed `text-destructive-strong` default for small text. Add a lint check (eslint-plugin-tailwindcss or a grep in CI) that flags text-(success|warning|info|attention|destructive) without -strong on text elements. Add Button variants `success` and `danger` with correct foregrounds and remove the ad-hoc bg-success classNames.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. StatusBadge FAMILY_CLASSES (lines 16-21), the badge.tsx variants (13-19), WorkItemRow:57 ('bg-warning/15 text-warning' at text-tiny) and globals.css:88-92 all use base 500-level tokens as text. globals.css says outright that -strong is the text shade. Token values are success 142 71% 45%, warning 38 92% 50%, attention 25 95% 53% and info 221 83% 53%, all below 4.5:1 on white. Grep counts: about 146 base-text uses against 37 -strong, in line with the finding. The bg-success buttons in LedgerTab:339 and :701 inherit the default variant's near-white primary-foreground. BoardClient and ApprovalsTab correctly add text-success-foreground. One correction to the recommendation: text-destructive-strong already exists (tailwind.config.js destructive.strong; used in cases/page.tsx:229), so nothing needs to be added; the work is migrating usages.

### #54 [P1] Server-action errors replace the whole screen; feedback comes in four different forms
*Area:* design-system · *Kind:* usability · *Effort:* L · *Personas:* rm, kam, accounts, founder_admin, board_member

**Problem.** There is no shared contract for action results. cases/actions.ts, billing-actions.ts, collections/actions.ts and policy/actions.ts mostly throw on business-rule violations, for example 'Only finished cases can be archived'. Settings and admin actions return {success:false}. 33 forms bind server actions directly (<form action={fn}>) with no try/catch. A rule violation therefore propagates to the nearest error boundary, and the user's whole working screen is replaced by 'Something went wrong!', losing any input. In production builds Next.js also redacts the thrown message. ConfirmDialog awaits the action and has no error state, so a failed delete or archive either blows up the page or closes silently. Successes are just as inconsistent. sonner toasts are used in 5 files, browser alert() in 5, inline banners in a few tab components, and policy deletes and saves give no feedback at all. The whole app has only 7 aria-live/role=status/role=alert regions.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/cases/actions.ts:40 — throws 'Only finished cases can be archived…'; src/app/cases/[id]/billing-actions.ts (42 throws, 0 result returns); src/app/policy/actions.ts (44 throws)`
- `src/components/creditflow/ConfirmDialog.tsx:81-84 — `await action(formData); onOpenChange(false);` with no catch and no error display`
- `src/app/error.tsx:19-29 — generic 'Something went wrong!' at h-screen, nested inside the Shell's scrolling <main>`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:382; src/app/collections/CollectionsClient.tsx:451; src/app/policy/validity/ValidityClient.tsx:31; src/app/policy/routing/RoutingClient.tsx:39; src/app/reset-password/page.tsx:39 — alert()`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx, src/app/settings/DelayReasonManager.tsx, src/components/admin/PartyDialog.tsx — the only sonner toast users; src/app/layout.tsx:27 Toaster`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/board/BoardClient.tsx:203 — vote form action={submitBoardVote} with no result handling`

**Recommendation.** Define `type ActionResult = {ok:true; receipt?:{title, detail, nextOwner}} | {ok:false; error:string; field?:string}` and convert throwing actions to return it. Reserve throws for auth and invariant failures. Ship a `useServerAction` hook plus an `<ActionForm>` primitive that keeps input on error, shows the inline error (focused, aria-live), and on success shows a role='status' receipt and an optional toast. Update ConfirmDialog to keep the dialog open and show the error inside it. Replace every alert() with this pattern. Rewrite the error.tsx copy to say what failed, that no data was changed, and include a digest reference.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. cases/actions.ts:40 throws the archive rule error. billing-actions.ts has 42 'throw new Error' and policy/actions.ts has 44. ConfirmDialog.tsx:81-84 awaits action() with no catch or error state. Root error.tsx uses h-screen with a generic 'Something went wrong!'. alert() appears at the five cited spots. sonner is imported only in AdminClient, the Rca/DelayReasonManagers, PartyDialog and layout. BoardClient:203 binds submitBoardVote directly, and that action ignores the upsert error (board/actions.ts:102-108), so a failed vote can look successful. Grep finds 7 aria-live/role=status/alert regions. Partly handled: LedgerTab's wrap() helper (lines 158-164) catches errors into inline state for the flows that use it, but write-off and credit-note approval bypass it. The recommendation is sound.

### #55 [P1] Failed queries show as 'nothing to do': empty, failed and zero states are merged
*Area:* design-system · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* ordinary_approver, board_member, rm, kam, accounts, founder_admin

**Problem.** Only the case list checks query errors. My Work does `res.data || []` for every queue. If Supabase errors or RLS rejects the query, an approver sees 'No open decision rounds need your vote' and a board member sees 'No open board votes'. That false all-clear means a decision or ballot can be missed. The audit page shows 'No events yet.' on failure. The EmptyState component, which has a `kind` prop for exactly this distinction, is used in one place and `kind` is never passed anywhere. There are about 37 hand-rolled 'No X…' paragraphs, each with different styling. Where the case list does show an error, it prints the raw database message and a migration filename to business users.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/page.tsx:295-327 — recentRes.data || [], myTasksRes.data || [], draftsRes, returnedRes, approvedRes, awaitingRes… with no .error checks`
- `src/app/page.tsx:604-605 — approver empty copy 'No open decision rounds need your vote.' shown even when the query failed`
- `src/app/audit/page.tsx:26 — 'No events yet.' with no error branch`
- `src/components/creditflow/EmptyState.tsx:22, :30-32 — kind prop exists; grep shows it is used only in src/app/cases/page.tsx:240 and kind is never passed`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:227-234 — shows 'Database error: {queryError.message}' and a migration filename`

**Recommendation.** Create a `<QueueState data error emptyText>` (or extend EmptyState) that renders a distinct 'Couldn't load — retry' state with a Retry action whenever error is set, and use it for every My Work queue, audit, search and collections. Add a reusable `<DataValue value state>` for single fields (Not provided, Not applicable, Unavailable). Show business-language error copy with technical detail in a collapsed details block visible to admins only.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified. page.tsx:294-346 uses .data || [] for every queue and destructures only { data } for the approver and board queries, with no .error checks. Line 570 renders 'No open decision rounds need your vote.' and line 591 'No open board votes.' (line numbers differ slightly from the evidence; the behavior is the same). audit/page.tsx shows 'No events yet.' with no error branch. EmptyState has kind='failed' but its only caller (cases/page.tsx:242) never passes kind. cases/page.tsx:227-234 prints 'Database error: {message}' plus a migration filename. A false all-clear on approver and board queues can hide a pending decision, so P1 is right.

### #56 [P1] The same board ballot follows different rules and controls on two screens; destructive and approve styling drifts
*Area:* design-system · *Kind:* consistency · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* board_member, ordinary_approver, founder_admin

**Problem.** A board member can vote from the dedicated board page or from the Decision tab, and the rules differ. On the board page the rationale is required, Reject is a filled red button, the buttons have no pending state, and an existing vote can be changed ('Current vote: …'). In the Decision tab the rationale is optional, Reject is an outline button, and the copy says 'Contact the founder if you need to revise it'. One governance act should not have two policies for rationale and revision. Destructive styling is also inconsistent across the app. ConfirmDialog uses outline red, the board page uses filled red, Withdraw uses outline red, and Log Out is a red destructive-styled button on every page, where it competes with real warnings. Approve buttons come from six copies of ad-hoc bg-success classNames. The intake wizard uses the legacy .btn-primary and .btn-secondary raw buttons instead of the Button primitive.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/cases/[id]/board/BoardClient.tsx:207-221 — rationale `required`, filled destructive Reject, plain Buttons with no pending; :195-198 'Current vote' implies the vote can be revised`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/ApprovalsTab.tsx:514-529 — rationale has no `required`, outline Reject; :531-533 'Contact the founder if you need to revise it'`
- `src/components/creditflow/ConfirmDialog.tsx:127-128 — destructive confirm is outline red`
- `src/components/Shell.tsx:191-195 — Log Out button styled text-destructive with border-destructive/20 on every screen`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/ApprovalsTab.tsx:330, :522; src/app/cases/[id]/board/BoardClient.tsx:213; src/app/cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:339, :701 — ad-hoc bg-success approve buttons`
- `src/app/cases/new/NewCaseForm.tsx:354, :543, :668 — legacy className='btn-primary'/'btn-secondary' raw <button>; src/app/globals.css:80-85`

**Recommendation.** Extract one `<BallotForm>` used by both screens, with a single rule set: rationale always required and a defined revision policy enforced server-side. Add Button variants `approve` and `danger` (filled, for the final committing action only) and `danger-outline` (for opening a confirm), then remove the ad-hoc classNames. Move Log Out into a user/role menu as a neutral item. Migrate NewCaseForm to Button and delete the legacy .btn-* classes.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified, and worse than described. There are two separate server actions with divergent behavior. submitBoardVote (board/actions.ts:81) upserts and ignores the upsert error; its rationale field is `required` client-side only; Reject uses variant='destructive'; its buttons are plain with no pending state. handleBoardVote (cases/[id]/actions.ts:883) has an optional comment and copies the comment into the audit description, which may contradict the 'revealed after closure' promise. Its UI hides the form once myVote exists ('Contact the founder…'), while the board page allows revision, and both actions upsert. Shell.tsx:191 styles Log Out with text-destructive. NewCaseForm uses .btn-primary and .btn-secondary raw buttons. ConfirmDialog's destructive button is an outline. The recommendation is sound: one BallotForm plus one server action, with the revision policy enforced server-side.

### #57 [P1] Failed data loads look like empty or zero results on My Work, Collections, Policy, Search and Settings
*Area:* live-sweep · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin, rm, kam, accounts

**Problem.** In the live session Supabase was unreachable. /cases showed 'Database error: TypeError: fetch failed'. In the same session, as founder_admin at 1024px: / showed Outstanding exposure ₹0, 'No write-offs or credit notes waiting on your decision' and 'No cases yet'; /collections showed '0 cases · ₹0 outstanding' and 'All clear. Nothing overdue right now 🎉'; /policy and every policy subpage said 'No policy version exists yet — use + New Draft'; /search said 'No matching cases'; /settings Risk controls showed only a warning banner. An admin could conclude the book is clean, skip collections calls, or create a duplicate policy draft when a live one exists. This breaks doctrine §13.6, which says 'failed' and 'empty' must look different.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/page.tsx:294-300 — recentRes, myTasksRes and doneTasksRes errors are dropped (`.data || []`)`
- `src/app/page.tsx:30-33 — a failed query falls into `totalExposure: 0`, so the card shows ₹0 rather than 'unavailable'`
- `src/app/collections/page.tsx:45 — `const { data: cases } = await casesQuery;` ignores the error`
- `src/app/collections/CollectionsClient.tsx:786-787 — the 'All clear … 🎉' empty state appears whenever the list is empty`
- `src/app/policy/actions.ts:57-62 — resolvePolicyVersion returns null on error; PolicyContextBar.tsx:17-21 then says no version exists`
- `src/app/settings/page.tsx:70 — `(settingsRes.data || []).map` gives an empty tab and no message`
- `Contrast: src/app/cases/page.tsx:227-238 handles the error correctly`

**Recommendation.** Build one shared `<LoadFailed what=… onRetry>` component and the rule that goes with it: every server query returns `{data, error}`, and any page or card whose source errored shows 'Couldn't load X — Retry' instead of a zero, 'All clear' or 'No policy version'. Metric cards must show 'Unavailable' (doctrine §13.6), never ₹0, when a query failed. Add a Playwright check that forces a Supabase failure and asserts there is no '₹0' or 'All clear' text.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified: page.tsx:294-300 uses `.data || []` with errors dropped; computeRmPortfolioMetrics (l.29-32) returns totalExposure 0 when cases is null; collections/page.tsx:45 destructures only data; CollectionsClient 786-792 'All clear 🎉' on empty; resolvePolicyVersion (actions.ts:47-63) ignores error -> PolicyContextBar 'No policy version exists'; settings/page.tsx:70 silent; search/page.tsx:38-39,77 also ignore errors. cases/page.tsx:227-238 is the correct contrast. Behavior is real, not a bypass artifact (the bypass only exposed it). Partial-schema failures are realistic (cases page already handles a missing archived_at migration, and home recentQuery filters archived_at). Downgraded P0->P1: it requires a backend/query failure and does not directly drive a credit approval, though 'All clear' on Collections could cause skipped follow-ups. Recommendation sound.

### #58 [P1] Founder home metrics: 'Approval success' is always blank, and 'Outstanding' and 'Average margin' don't compute what they claim
*Area:* live-sweep · *Kind:* bug · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* founder_admin, rm

**Problem.** The admin portfolio header says '· all cases'. The approval-success query still filters `.eq('rm_user_id', rmUserId)` with rmUserId = null, so no rows match and admins always see 'Not available'. The Outstanding card's definition says 'adjusted by approved credit notes', but the code computes promised minus actual and ignores credit notes, so exposure is overstated after a credit note. 'Average margin' is collected ÷ decided − 1 across Billing Active cases. Any case that is part-way through collection reads as a large negative 'margin' and is painted destructive red. Collection progress is being presented as a profitability problem.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/page.tsx:148-152 — `.eq('rm_user_id', rmUserId)` runs even when rmUserId is null (admin)`
- `src/app/page.tsx:689-692 — label says 'Portfolio metrics · all cases' for admin`
- `src/app/page.tsx:36-41 — exposure = promised − actual, no credit notes`
- `src/lib/vocabulary.ts:213 — the Outstanding definition promises a credit-note adjustment`
- `src/app/page.tsx:57-59, 710-716 — 'margin' = actual/decided − 1, shown in danger tone below 0`
- `Live: / as founder_admin at 1024px — all four cards say 'Not available' or ₹0, and 'Not available' at text-3xl wraps onto 2 lines in a 4-column grid`

**Recommendation.** Apply the rm_user_id filter only when rmUserId is set. Either include approved credit notes in the exposure calculation or change the definition text to match the code. Rename 'Average margin' to 'Collection realisation', limit it to Closed cases, and show it as a percentage of the decided amount with a neutral tone. Change 'Not available' to 'No data yet' with a smaller font size so the 4-column grid doesn't wrap.

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Approval-success bug confirmed: page.tsx:148-152 runs `.eq('rm_user_id', rmUserId)` unconditionally, and l.363 passes null for admin, so admin always gets no rows -> 'Not available'. Margin confirmed: l.56-58 computes actual/decided-1 across Billing Active/Pending/Closed/Cancelled cases with actual_bill_amount != null, so in-progress collection reads as negative and is toned danger (l.711-716). REFUTED sub-claim: credit notes ARE reflected. billing-actions.ts:604-612 (handleCreditNoteApproval) subtracts reduction_amount from promised_bill_amount on approval, so promised - actual already matches the GLOSSARY definition. Drop the 'include credit notes' recommendation. The 'Not available' wrapping was seen live on empty data, but the text-3xl string is code-determined so the cosmetic point stands.

### #59 [P1] The home 'Exceptions needing your authority' links lead nowhere useful
*Area:* live-sweep · *Kind:* bug · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** The founder's top exception card has a 'N credit notes pending → Review' row that links to /admin. /admin has four tabs (Party Master, Users & Roles, Committee Roster, Global Audit Log) and no credit-note screen, so the admin cannot act on what the home page flagged. The write-off row links to /cases?phase=billing, which lists every billing-phase case instead of only those pending write-off. MetricCard's own comment says links should go to 'the exact underlying queue — never to an unfiltered list'.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/page.tsx:627-633 — credit notes row href="/admin"`
- `src/app/admin/AdminClient.tsx:100-105 — no credit notes tab; grep finds no 'credit' in AdminClient.tsx`
- `src/app/page.tsx:619-625 — write-offs row href="/cases?phase=billing" (phase filter, not status)`
- `src/components/creditflow/MetricCard.tsx:10-11 — the stated linking rule`

**Recommendation.** Build a credit-note approval queue (an /admin tab with a deep link such as /admin?tab=credit-notes, or a filtered /cases view), or link each pending note to its case's Ledger tab. Link write-offs to /cases?status=Pending%20Write-Off%20Approval. Make the admin tabs controlled by the URL so links can target a tab.

*Verification:* confirmed: page.tsx:627-633 credit notes row links to /admin. AdminClient tabs are parties/users/roster/audit with no credit-note screen. Approval UI exists only in cases/[id]/LedgerTab.tsx:697-705, so the admin has to hunt through cases. Write-offs link /cases?phase=billing (l.620). cases/page.tsx:79-80 already supports `status=` exact filtering, so /cases?status=Pending%20Write-Off%20Approval works today with no new code. MetricCard.tsx comment confirms the stated rule. Recommendation sound.

### #60 [P1] The Add Party dialog drops the Credit Limit the user types and defaults to the wrong party type
*Area:* live-sweep · *Kind:* bug · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* rm, kam, founder_admin

**Problem.** From intake step 1 'Customer Party · Add New', the dialog shows a 'Credit Limit (₹)' field. upsertParty never reads `credit_limit`, so the value is silently lost. The new party then has no limit, and the intake's credit-limit check never applies to it. If the field were wired up, an RM could set the limit on a party they then immediately request credit for. The dialog was opened from the Customer field but defaults to Party Type 'Both' with Influencer Subtype 'Contractor' (seen live), so the RM has to fix fields they never meant to touch. On the edit path, update errors are ignored and the dialog reports success.

**Evidence.**
- `src/components/admin/PartyDialog.tsx:176-178 — <Input name="credit_limit" …> (also reads editingParty?.credit_limit, while the column is credit_line_amount)`
- `src/app/admin/actions.ts:27-39 — payload has no credit limit; line 41 `await supabase.from('parties').update(payload)` has no error check`
- `src/app/admin/parties/[id]/credit-line-actions.ts:12 — the real credit_line_amount write path`
- `src/app/cases/new/NewCaseForm.tsx:466-471, 889-893 — the dialog opens with no party-type context`
- `src/components/admin/PartyDialog.tsx:22 — `useState(editingParty?.party_type || 'both')``
- `Live: /cases/new as founder_admin, Add New → dialog shows Party Type 'Both' and a Credit Limit field`

**Recommendation.** Remove Credit Limit from the quick-add dialog, or send it through the credit-line action restricted to admins. Pass a `defaultType` prop ('customer' or 'contractor') from the intake button and hide the influencer fields for customers. Check the update error and show it. Connect each Label to its Input with htmlFor/id.

*Verification:* confirmed: PartyDialog.tsx:176-178 renders credit_limit (defaultValue editingParty?.credit_limit, but the column is credit_line_amount per NewCaseForm 483). admin/actions.ts:27-39 payload omits it; l.41 update has no error check and returns success. NewCaseForm 889-893 renders <PartyDialog> with no type prop, although partyTypeForDialog state exists (l.63, set at 468/507), so it is tracked and not passed. PartyDialog l.22 defaults 'both'/'contractor'. Not a bypass artifact. Severity: borderline P1/P2. It is a silent data drop on a credit-control field, so P1 is kept.

### #61 [P1] Intake work is lost on in-app navigation; the 'unsaved work' guard only covers closing the tab
*Area:* live-sweep · *Kind:* usability · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* rm, founder_admin

**Problem.** NewCaseForm protects work only with a beforeunload listener. Clicking any sidebar link (My Work, Cases, Collections), the header search or Log Out is a client-side transition, which does not fire beforeunload. An RM halfway through a 4-step intake with tranches and intake answers loses everything with no warning. On mobile, the icon-only Log Out button sits right next to the notification bell, which makes this easy to hit by accident.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/cases/new/NewCaseForm.tsx:53-61 — beforeunload only`
- `src/components/Shell.tsx:109-112 — next/link nav items (client transitions)`
- `src/components/Shell.tsx:190-196 — signOut form; on mobile it is an icon-only button next to the bell`
- `Live: /cases/new at 375px — the Log Out icon is adjacent to the bell in the header`

**Recommendation.** Short term: add a route-change guard (intercept Link clicks while hasContentRef is true and show a 'Leave intake? Save draft / Discard / Stay' dialog). Better: autosave a server draft after step 1 and debounce updates, so 'Save draft & exit' becomes 'Exit'. This is what doctrine Principle 11 asks for: drafting should reduce commitment, not postpone saving.

*Verification:* confirmed: NewCaseForm.tsx:53-61 only listens to beforeunload. Shell.tsx sidebar uses next/link (client transitions), and Log Out is a server-action form (l.189-195). Neither fires beforeunload. hasContentRef is computed at l.176. A Save draft button exists (saveDraftButton), but nothing prompts the user to use it. On mobile, Log Out is icon-only (sm:inline label) next to NotificationDropdown. Recommendation sound.

### #62 [P1] Grades are worked out by three different resolvers, and intake grades are trusted from the client
*Area:* backlog-status · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* rm, founder_admin, ordinary_approver

**Problem.** Item 1 is fixed, but the fix is spread across three copies of the band logic that disagree. The intake client maps link_list, yes_no, numeric and date, but not legacy 'dropdown'. The server-side task completion maps numeric, dropdown, link_list and yes_no, but not date. Intake grade_value is calculated in the browser, stored in case_attributes.draft_rm_answers, and copied as-is into stage_tasks when the case is submitted. Nothing re-derives it on the server. So an RM draft saved under policy v1 and submitted after v2 is published keeps v1 grades, a legacy dropdown parameter answered at intake gets no grade, and a tampered payload can set any grade.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/cases/new/NewCaseForm.tsx:244-272 - client-side band/mapping logic. Line 263 covers only link_list|yes_no, so 'dropdown' is missing`
- `src/app/cases/[id]/actions.ts:322-332 - server logic has numeric and dropdown but no 'date' branch`
- `src/utils/engine.ts:306-320 - draft answer grade_value is copied directly into stage_tasks`
- `src/app/cases/new/actions.ts:105 - the client grade is also written to party_parameter_values for reuse on future cases`

**Recommendation.** Create one pure helper, resolveGrade(param, rawInput, gradeScale), in src/utils/scoring and use it in all three places. In generateStageTasks, ignore the client grade_value and re-resolve from raw_input_value against the cycle's policy_snapshot_id parameter definitions. If the mapping changed, mark the task Pending with a 'Policy changed — re-confirm' note. Add unit tests for every input_type.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified NewCaseForm.tsx:244-272: branches for numeric, date and link_list|yes_no, with no 'dropdown'. Server actions.ts:322-332: numeric and dropdown|link_list|yes_no, with no 'date'. engine.ts:306-320 copies answer.grade_value from case_attributes.draft_rm_answers unchanged, and cases/new/actions.ts:105 persists the client grade to party_parameter_values. There is no server-side re-derivation, so the policy-drift and tampering concerns are real. The recommendation (a shared pure resolveGrade, re-resolved server-side against policy_snapshot_id) is sound.

### #63 [P1] Policy parameters list doesn't update after create/edit/delete, and save errors are swallowed
*Area:* backlog-status · *Kind:* bug · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** ParametersClient copies initialParams into useState once and never calls setParams. After upsertParameter or deleteParameter revalidates the page, the server passes fresh props but the table keeps the old list. A new parameter doesn't appear and a deleted one stays, which invites duplicate creation. The dialog also closes on onSubmit before the server action finishes. Server-side validation errors, such as 'Yes/No parameters require both Yes and No grade mappings' or a locked-version assert, therefore give no in-context feedback. Deleting a parameter is a single click with no confirmation, even though it archives a scoring input. GradesClient uses the same stale-state pattern.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/policy/parameters/ParametersClient.tsx:39 - useState(initialParams); setParams is never used`
- `src/app/policy/parameters/ParametersClient.tsx:241 - onSubmit={() => setOpen(false)} closes before the action completes`
- `src/app/policy/parameters/ParametersClient.tsx:223-226 - delete form submits immediately with no confirm`
- `src/app/policy/actions.ts:361-366,371,374 - throws that the user never sees`
- `src/app/policy/grades/GradesClient.tsx:24 - same useState(initialGrades) pattern`

**Recommendation.** Render straight from props (drop the local copy), or sync with useEffect / a key on the version. Wrap the dialog submit in useTransition. Make the action return { error } instead of throwing, keep the dialog open, show the error inline, and close only on success with a toast such as 'Parameter saved to draft v3'. Add a confirm dialog for delete that names the parameter, its weight and its stage, and states the capacity change.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified ParametersClient.tsx:39: setParams is declared and never called, and there is no useEffect or key. The page (parameters/page.tsx) passes initialParams with no key, so revalidatePath's new props are ignored. GradesClient.tsx:24 has the same issue (setGrades unused). Line 241 has onSubmit={() => setOpen(false)}, and the delete form at 223-226 submits immediately. Correction: thrown errors are not fully swallowed. They bubble to the global src/app/error.tsx (there is no policy-level boundary), so the user gets a generic error page and loses the form input, not inline feedback. The UX problem stands.

### #64 [P1] Policy simulation is still a stub with its own formula, so it can disagree with production
*Area:* backlog-status · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* founder_admin, board_member

**Problem.** Item 6 is OPEN. runSimulation returns { simulated: true } and is imported but never called. SimulationClient re-implements dominance and band mapping in the browser, and that code has already drifted from the engine: power_law there has no exponent>0 guard and clamps only the contractor score with Math.max(x,1), while scoring.ts clamps both and guards the exponent. The simulation also ignores persona minimum_score gates, missing critical evidence and routing depth, all of which now affect live outcomes. The props parameters, grades and personas, and the exposure state, are unused, so the screen suggests a richer test than it runs. An admin can publish a policy believing a 72 lands in band X while live cases show 'ambiguous → board'.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/policy/simulation/actions.ts:2 - five scoring imports, none used`
- `src/app/policy/simulation/actions.ts:5-9 - stub returns { simulated: true }`
- `src/app/policy/simulation/SimulationClient.tsx:7 - runSimulation is imported but never called`
- `src/app/policy/simulation/SimulationClient.tsx:9,16 - unused parameters/grades/personas props and exposure state`
- `src/app/policy/simulation/SimulationClient.tsx:26-27 vs src/utils/scoring.ts:179-185 - power-law math differs`
- `src/app/policy/simulation/SimulationClient.tsx:107-111 - shows only band ambiguity, not critical/persona reasons`

**Recommendation.** Move combine-scores, map-to-band and ambiguity-reasons into pure functions in utils/scoring that take plain data. Call them from both updateCycleScore and a real runSimulation(versionId, inputs) server action. Let the admin choose personas and exposure/scenario, and show the outcome in business terms: final score, band, credit days, required stage plus the routing reason, and ambiguity reasons. Add 'replay the last 50 decided cases against this draft' to show the impact before publishing.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified simulation/actions.ts: five unused imports and a stub that returns { simulated: true }. SimulationClient imports runSimulation without calling it. The parameters/grades/personas props and the exposure state are unused. Power-law at :27 has no exponent guard and clamps only the contractor score with Math.max(x,1), whereas scoring.ts:179-185 guards exp>0 and clamps both to 0-100. The simulation also rounds to 2 decimals and does nothing for a missing dominance row, unlike the engine's scenarioDefaultScore fallback. The result card shows only band ambiguity, and updateCycleScore's own comment says the two must match. Recommendation is sound.

### #65 [P1] Stage max drift warning combines both subjects and gives the wrong advice when capacity is below max
*Area:* backlog-status · *Kind:* clarity · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* founder_admin

**Problem.** The new drift warning sums weight × topGrade over all parameters up to the stage, mixing customer and contractor parameters. Scoring normalises each subject separately against the same stage max_total. The implied capacity is therefore overstated (roughly doubled when both subjects are configured), which pushes admins to raise max_total. That compresses every score and moves cases between bands. Persona weight overrides are also ignored. And when configured > implied, the copy still says 'scores will cap', although the real effect is that scores can never reach 100.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/policy/stages/page.tsx:14-19 - impliedTotals ignores subject_type and persona overrides`
- `src/utils/scoring.ts:60-62 - subject score uses only that subject's parameters`
- `src/utils/scoring.ts:110-121 - one max_total per stage normalises each subject`
- `src/app/policy/stages/StagesClient.tsx:83-86 - one message for both drift directions`

**Recommendation.** Work out capacity per subject, and per persona when overrides exist, and show a small table for each stage: customer capacity, contractor capacity, configured max. Use direction-specific copy: 'Max 400 but parameters reach 450 — strong cases cap at 100' versus 'Max 400 but parameters reach only 320 — best possible score is 80'. Offer a one-click 'Set max to capacity' fix.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified stages/page.tsx:14-19: it sums weight*topGrade over all parameters with stage<=N, with no subject_type split and no persona overrides. scoring.ts filters by subject (:60-62) and normalises each subject against the same max_total (:110-121). With both subjects configured, the implied capacity is about double. StagesClient.tsx:85-86 uses Math.abs drift and a single 'scores will cap' message, which is wrong when configured exceeds implied. Following this advice would compress all scores and shift bands. Recommendation is sound.

### #66 [P2] The amount on each row means different things with no label
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* clarity · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* ordinary_approver, kam, rm, accounts, board_member

**Problem.** WorkItemRow prints a bare compact INR figure. On task, RM, KAM and board rows it is bill_amount. On approver rows it is requested_exposure_amount, falling back silently to bill_amount. On upcoming-collection rows it is the unpaid tranche. The case list always shows bill_amount labelled 'bill', even for Billing Active cases where the user cares about outstanding, and for approved cases where approved terms are shown but not the approved amount. An approver comparing two decisions cannot tell whether ₹50L is the exposure being asked for or the gross bill. This is exactly the requested-vs-approved confusion that Principle 4 forbids.

**Evidence.**
- `src/components/creditflow/WorkItemRow.tsx:46-48 — amount rendered without a label`
- `src/app/page.tsx:578 — amount={c?.requested_exposure_amount || c?.bill_amount}`
- `src/app/page.tsx:653 — amount={t.unpaid} in the same slot`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:292 — '{bill_amount} bill' regardless of lifecycle phase`
- `src/components/creditflow/TaskKanban.tsx:44 — bill amount again with no label`

**Recommendation.** Add a required amountLabel prop to WorkItemRow ('Requested', 'Bill', 'Due', 'Outstanding', 'Approved') and render it as '₹12.4L requested'. In the case list, pick the value by phase: requested exposure in review/decision, approved amount after approval, outstanding (promised − collected) in billing. Never fall back between layers without saying so. Show the exact amount in a title or tooltip next to the compact value (§13.2).

*Verification:* confirmed: WorkItemRow.tsx:46-48 renders a bare formatCompactINR with no label. Approver rows use requested_exposure_amount || bill_amount (page.tsx:578), and upcoming rows use t.unpaid. TaskKanban also shows an unlabelled bill amount. The cases list does label it as 'bill', but always bill_amount regardless of phase. Downgraded to P2 because approvers decide on the case page decision brief, which carries labelled layers, so the home row is triage context and not the decision surface. The recommendation is sound.

### #67 [P2] The case list is a card feed, not a retrieval tool: no sort, owner, SLA or amount filters
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* efficiency · *Effort:* L · *Personas:* founder_admin, kam, accounts, ordinary_approver, board_member, bdo, rm

**Problem.** The list is fixed to updated_at desc with 25 cards per page. Filters are only a phase chip and text search. A 'status' param exists in the URL but has no UI. None of the filters doctrine 12.2 asks for exist: owner/assignee, RM, KAM, stage, amount range, scenario, SLA state, waiting reason, date ranges. Admin, accounts, BDO, approvers and board see every case unscoped, with no 'Mine' or 'Assigned to me' toggle. Three-line cards with left-aligned amounts cannot be scanned or compared. The right column shows RM but never KAM, so a KAM cannot spot their colleagues' cases. Toggling 'Include archived' does nothing until Search is pressed.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:67-68 — hard-coded .order('updated_at') with no sort param`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:25 — searchParams accept status but no control sets it`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:70-73 — scoping only for rm/kam; other roles get everything`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:252-327 — card layout with 3 text lines per case`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:306-309 — right column shows RM and opened date only`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:189-199 — archived checkbox only takes effect on submit`

**Recommendation.** Replace the cards on lg+ screens with a dense table: Case · Party · Phase/Status · Amount (labelled by phase, right-aligned, tabular-nums) · Stage · Next action/Owner · SLA/Age · Last activity. Make columns sortable through ?sort=. Add a filter bar for RM, KAM, owner, stage, SLA (breached/due ≤3d), amount range and scenario, all held in the URL. Default the view by role ('Assigned to me' for bdo/accounts, 'My decisions' for approvers). Let users save a filter set as a named view. Keep the cards below md.

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Confirmed: hard-coded order('updated_at') at line 67, the status param has no control (only cleared by the chip links), scoping exists only for rm/kam, the card layout, and the archived checkbox needs a submit (it is a form field with form='case-filters'). Overstated: line 3 does show the KAM name as owner whenever the status ownerRole is 'kam' (lines 258-260 and 303), so 'never KAM' is inaccurate. It only shows the KAM when the KAM owns the next step. Severity lowered: this is a retrieval-efficiency improvement and does not block a core job, since search and phase filters exist.

### #68 [P2] Opportunity: a ranked 'Top priorities today' strip instead of fixed-order sections
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* opportunity · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* rm, kam, ordinary_approver, accounts, founder_admin

**Problem.** My Work is a two-column grid of sections in hard-coded order. 'Tasks assigned to you' always comes first, even for RMs and approvers who rarely get stage tasks, so an RM's red 'Returned for revision' card can fall below the fold on a laptop. Nothing ranks work across queues by SLA breach, exposure or broken promises, which is the doctrine's design test ('identify the three most important actions without opening multiple cases'). The same tasks are drawn twice: in the queue section and again in the Kanban below it, at the cost of vertical space and attention.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/page.tsx:431-676 — fixed section order; tasks section first for all roles (434-459)`
- `src/app/page.tsx:679-686 — TaskKanban repeats myTasks already listed at 440-458`
- `src/app/page.tsx:296-300 — tasks sorted only by sla_deadline; no exposure weighting`

**Recommendation.** Build a server-side priority list: normalise every queue item to {kind, caseId, dueAt, overdueDays, exposure, severity} and score it by breached SLA > broken PTP > due within 48h > exposure > age. Render the top 3-5 as a 'Do these first' strip under the header, each with a one-line reason and a primary action. Order the remaining sections by role (returned work first for RM, decisions first for approver). Collapse the Kanban behind a 'Board view' toggle on the tasks section instead of showing a duplicate list.

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Confirmed: fixed section order with the tasks section first for all roles (434-459), TaskKanban repeating myTasks (679-686), and sort by sla_deadline only. Overstated: on lg the grid is 2 columns, so the RM 'Returned for revision' card renders top-right, beside tasks, not below the fold. The fold claim applies only below lg. The claim that RMs and approvers 'rarely get stage tasks' is unverified. As an opportunity this rates P2, not P1.

### #69 [P2] Home counts and 'View all' links don't match the lists they open
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* trust · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* rm, kam, ordinary_approver, founder_admin, bdo, accounts

**Problem.** Section counts are the length of capped arrays: limit(10) for RM and KAM queues, limit(20) for approver rounds before the my-vote filter, limit(50) for tasks of which 8 are shown. The badge reads '(10)' when there are 40 drafts. 'View all' on tasks goes to /cases, which cannot filter by task. KAM 'In review' counts In Review plus Awaiting Input, but its link goes to phase=review, which is In Review only. Admin 'write-offs pending' links to phase=billing (every billing case), credit notes link to /admin generically, and each upcoming-collection row links to /collections instead of its case. The KAM overdue banner scopes to kam_user_id = me, while /collections also includes unassigned cases, so the numbers disagree. Home looks 14 days ahead and Collections 30. 'Recent activity on your cases' is unscoped for every role except RM and KAM, so admin, approvers, BDO and Accounts see all cases under a 'your cases' label.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/page.tsx:312-314, 323-325 — limit(10); count={array.length} at 464, 479, 494, 521, 535, 550`
- `src/app/page.tsx:335-339 — limit(20) then filter`
- `src/app/page.tsx:438 — tasks viewAllHref='/cases'`
- `src/app/page.tsx:323 vs 535 and src/lib/vocabulary.ts:184 — count/link status mismatch`
- `src/app/page.tsx:621, 629, 650 — generic destinations`
- `src/app/page.tsx:355 vs src/app/collections/page.tsx:41 — KAM scope mismatch`
- `src/app/page.tsx:255-259, 663 — 'your cases' unscoped for non-RM/KAM`

**Recommendation.** Use count:'exact', head:true queries for badge counts and label partial lists ('Showing 10 of 40'). Point each 'View all' at a list filtered exactly the same way: /cases?status=Pending%20Write-Off%20Approval, /cases?kam=me&phase=review,waiting, a new /cases?assignee=me task view, /admin?tab=credit-notes, and /cases/{id}?tab=financials for tranche rows. Share scope helpers between Home and Collections. Rename the recent-activity section 'Recently updated cases' for roles that aren't scoped.

*Verification:* confirmed: Verified: counts are capped array lengths (limit 10/20/50, with 8 tasks shown), and tasks 'View all' points to /cases. KAM 'In review' counts In Review plus Awaiting Input, but phase=review is ['In Review'] only (vocabulary.ts:184). Write-offs link to phase=billing, credit notes to /admin, and upcoming rows to /collections. The KAM tranche scope is kam_user_id=me (page.tsx:355), while collections/page.tsx:41 uses kam=me OR kam IS NULL. Home looks 14 days ahead and Collections 30 (collections/page.tsx:109). Recent-activity scoped() is a no-op for non-RM/KAM roles. Proposed /admin?tab=credit-notes was not verified to exist.

### #70 [P2] Due-label colours are inconsistent and contradictory, and text tokens skip the -strong variants
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* consistency · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* rm, kam, bdo, accounts

**Problem.** Every non-overdue dueLabel in WorkItemRow renders amber (bg-warning/15 text-warning), whether it says 'Valid for 30d', 'Waiting 2d', 'Returned 3d ago' or 'Due in 12d'. Amber stops meaning anything. A validity that has already lapsed ('Expired 3d ago') stays amber because overdue is never passed. A task paused with is_waiting shows 'Waiting — SLA paused' next to a red 'Overdue 4d' in the queue, while the Kanban suppresses overdue for the same task. Text colour uses base tokens (text-warning, text-success, text-destructive) on tinted backgrounds in WorkItemRow, MetricCard and the case list, against the codebase rule that text uses -strong variants for contrast. TaskKanban gets this right.

**Evidence.**
- `src/components/creditflow/WorkItemRow.tsx:53-61 — binary destructive/warning; no neutral tone`
- `src/app/page.tsx:508 — 'Expired Nd ago' without overdue flag`
- `src/app/page.tsx:442-454 — overdue computed even when t.is_waiting`
- `src/components/creditflow/TaskKanban.tsx:27 — Kanban excludes waiting tasks from overdue (inconsistent)`
- `src/components/creditflow/MetricCard.tsx:34-39 — text-warning/text-destructive/text-success`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:284, 294 — text-warning / text-success on tinted backgrounds`

**Recommendation.** Replace the overdue boolean with tone: 'neutral' | 'attention' | 'destructive', and set neutral by default for informational ages ('Returned 3d ago', 'Waiting 2d'), attention for due within 3 days, and destructive for breached or expired. Suppress overdue when is_waiting is true, in a shared helper used by both the queue and the Kanban. Switch all text on tinted backgrounds to text-*-strong. Add an icon next to colour so meaning doesn't rely on colour alone (§13.2).

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Confirmed: WorkItemRow only toggles between destructive and warning, the 'Expired Nd ago' row passes no overdue flag (page.tsx:508), the queue computes overdue even when is_waiting (442-454) while the Kanban suppresses it (TaskKanban.tsx:27), and MetricCard, WorkItemRow and cases/page.tsx:284/294 use base tokens on tints, against the bg-X/10 text-X-strong convention. Correction: TaskKanban does not fully 'get this right'. It uses text-warning-strong but plain text-destructive for overdue, so include it in the -strong sweep.

### #71 [P2] Role switcher: labelled 'Impersonate', no accessible name, fires a server action on every change
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* usability · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* founder_admin, rm, kam, ordinary_approver, board_member

**Problem.** Doctrine 6.7 describes a 'View as' context for multi-role users. The UI calls it 'Impersonate Role:', which suggests acting as someone else. It sits at the bottom of the sidebar, and on mobile it is reachable only through the drawer. The label is a <p>, so the <select> has no accessible name. Options are raw uppercase keys ('FOUNDER ADMIN', 'ORDINARY APPROVER') instead of ROLE_LABELS. onChange calls switchImpersonationRole and router.refresh straight away, so keyboard arrowing through options (which fires change in Chromium on Windows) switches role on every step. The control renders even for single-role users. There is no confirmation that the workspace changed, and the user stays on a page the new role's nav no longer offers (for example /policy after switching to RM). 'Operator' flashes before the session loads.

**Evidence.**
- `src/components/RoleSwitcher.tsx:38 — 'Impersonate Role:' as <p>, not <label htmlFor>`
- `src/components/RoleSwitcher.tsx:41-52 — server action plus refresh on every onChange`
- `src/components/RoleSwitcher.tsx:56 — r.replace('_',' ').toUpperCase() instead of ROLE_LABELS`
- `src/components/RoleSwitcher.tsx:33 — 'Operator' fallback`
- `src/components/Shell.tsx:148, 169 — rendered at the sidebar or drawer bottom only`

**Recommendation.** Move it to a header user menu: 'Viewing as: Key Account Manager ▾', using ROLE_LABELS, as a menu of buttons rather than a select. Hide it when user.roles.length < 2. On switch, navigate to / and show a toast ('Now viewing as KAM: your queues and nav have changed'). Show a skeleton instead of 'Operator'. Keep the union-of-roles authorization note in the menu's helper text.

*Verification:* confirmed: RoleSwitcher.tsx: <p>'Impersonate Role:'</p> is not a label, so the select has no accessible name. onChange calls switchImpersonationRole then router.refresh immediately. Options use r.replace('_',' ').toUpperCase(), not ROLE_LABELS. It shows the 'Operator' fallback and renders even with one role. Shell renders it only in the sidebar or the drawer. There is no navigation or toast after a switch. Nav is filtered by role, but the user stays on the current path (admin layouts would then call unauthorized()). The recommendation is sound.

### #72 [P2] Two search engines behave differently, and party counts don't match what the link opens
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* consistency · *Effort:* M · *Personas:* rm, kam, founder_admin, accounts

**Problem.** The header search (/search) matches case number, party name or code, and RM/KAM name, capped at 25 results with no 'see all'. The search box on /cases matches only case number and party, so typing a KAM's name gives results in one place and nothing in the other. The header input has no accessible label and clears itself after navigating, so the user can't refine the query. Party result counts are computed unscoped, while the party link opens /cases?party=, which is scoped to the RM or KAM. An RM sees 'Acme · 12 cases', clicks, and gets 2. The helper text promises the party page 'shows its cases, exposure and activity', but /cases shows no exposure or activity summary. There is no party profile destination (doctrine 11.3).

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/search/page.tsx:54-68 — RM/KAM-name matching, limit(25), no overflow link`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:89-96 — case number plus party only`
- `src/components/SearchBar.tsx:7, 20-26 — local state resets; no <label> or aria-label`
- `src/app/search/page.tsx:80-94 — unscoped party case counts (limit 200)`
- `src/app/search/page.tsx:149, 166 — link to /cases?party= and a promise of exposure/activity`

**Recommendation.** Move matching into one server function (searchCases(term, scope)) used by both surfaces. Show 'See all N results in Cases →' when capped. Prefill the header input from ?q= on /search and give it aria-label='Search cases and parties'. Scope party counts the same way /cases does. Point party results at /admin/parties/[id], or add a party header to /cases?party= with total exposure, outstanding and recent activity.

*Verification:* partially_confirmed: Confirmed: /search also matches RM/KAM names via profiles and caps at limit(25) with no overflow link, /cases matches only case number and party, party counts are unscoped (and also include archived cases, while /cases?party= excludes them by default), and the helper text promises 'exposure and activity' that /cases does not show. The input has no label or aria-label. Refuted detail: SearchBar does not clear itself. The state is never reset, and the component persists in the layout Shell, so the term stays. The real gap is that the input is not prefilled from ?q= when arriving via a link or reload.

### #73 [P2] Unauthorized and error states: 404 instead of Access Denied, and no route back
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* bug · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* rm, kam, bdo, accounts, ordinary_approver, board_member

**Problem.** Settings pages and actions call redirect('/unauthorized'), but no /unauthorized route exists. src/app/unauthorized.tsx is the authInterrupts file convention, which renders only when unauthorized() is called. So non-admins get a 404 instead of 'Access Denied'. The access-denied page says 'Return to Dashboard' while the nav calls it My Work, and it doesn't tell a multi-role user which role is needed or offer to switch view. error.tsx says 'Something went wrong!' with no error reference (digest) for support, no link to My Work, and h-screen inside the shell's main, as does unauthorized.tsx, which causes a double scrollbar. The case-list error banner shows raw database messages and a migration filename to business users. loading.tsx is one generic spinner for every route.

**Evidence.**
- `src/app/settings/page.tsx:20 and src/app/settings/actions.ts:54-141 — redirect('/unauthorized')`
- `src/app/unauthorized.tsx:1-22 — file convention, not a route; 'Return to Dashboard' at line 18; h-screen at line 7`
- `src/app/error.tsx:19-29 — generic copy, no digest, h-screen`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:230-236 — raw DB error and migration filename shown`
- `src/app/loading.tsx:3-11 — spinner only`

**Recommendation.** Replace redirect('/unauthorized') with forbidden()/unauthorized(), or add src/app/unauthorized/page.tsx. Rewrite the page as: 'This page needs Founder/Admin. You're viewing as RM.' with buttons [Switch view] (when the user holds the role) and [Go to My Work]. In error.tsx, show 'Reference: {digest}', a My Work link, and min-h-[50vh] instead of h-screen. Log raw DB errors to the server and show users 'The case list couldn't load. Try again or share reference X'. Add skeleton loading.tsx files for / and /cases that match the layout.

*Verification:* confirmed: There is no src/app/unauthorized/ route directory, only unauthorized.tsx (authInterrupts: true in next.config). settings/page.tsx:20 and settings/actions.ts (9 call sites) call redirect('/unauthorized'), which gives a 404. The other admin layouts correctly call unauthorized(). The page says 'Return to Dashboard' and uses h-screen. error.tsx uses generic copy, no digest and h-screen. cases/page.tsx:230-236 shows the raw DB message and the migration filename. loading.tsx is a single spinner. Note: for a role failure, forbidden() (403) is semantically better than unauthorized() (401), but it needs a forbidden.tsx.

### #74 [P2] Mobile drawer, notification panel and filter chips break keyboard and screen-reader use
*Area:* shell-home-nav · *Kind:* accessibility · *Effort:* S · *Personas:* rm, kam, board_member, ordinary_approver

**Problem.** The mobile drawer declares role='dialog' and aria-modal, but focus doesn't move into it, isn't trapped, and isn't returned to the menu button on close, so screen-reader users stay on the hidden page. The notification panel has the same problem and its items aren't a list. On /cases the phase chips are <Link><Button> (a button nested in an anchor, which is invalid interactive nesting), so each chip is announced twice and keyboard behaviour differs between browsers. The header search input has only a placeholder. Log Out is a permanent red outline button next to the bell: a routine action styled as destructive, which competes visually on every screen.

**Evidence.**
- `src/components/Shell.tsx:152-171 — dialog without focus management`
- `src/components/NotificationDropdown.tsx:85-144 — no focus move, no role=list`
- `src/app/cases/page.tsx:203-212, 214-223 — <Link><Button> nesting`
- `src/components/SearchBar.tsx:20-26 — no label`
- `src/components/Shell.tsx:190-196 — destructive-styled Log Out always visible`

**Recommendation.** Build the drawer with shadcn Sheet and the notifications with Popover, both Radix with built-in focus trap and return. Render the chips as <Button asChild variant=...><Link/></Button>. Add aria-label to the search. Move Log Out into the header user menu alongside the 'Viewing as' switcher and give it a neutral style.

*Verification:* confirmed: Shell.tsx drawer: role=dialog, aria-modal, Escape handler only, and no focus move, trap or return.
